For AMI, please see the response to Question 1 above.  For Tools & Equipment, program
and projects descriptions are provided in the whitepapers provided in Exhibit __ (GIOP-
4).
3.
With respect to AMI, as set forth in the Company’s AMI Panel’s direct testimony (pages
6, 9, and 10 of 31), the Company proposed a six-year AMI deployment program.  The
plan consists of two years of back-office system installation, followed by four years of
AMI meter and gas module deployment.  Please see Exhibit___(AMIP-1) and Exhibit
(AMIP-2) for further detail on the scope of work and schedule for each phase.  Please
also see Exhibit (AMIP-3), Schedules 2, 3, and 10 for additional information on the
deployment schedule and associated budgets.
For Tools & Equipment, detailed phased plans for the Rate Year are not available at this
time.  Programs/Blankets are phased based on historical spend during the work plan
cycle, which takes place in the fourth quarter of the previous fiscal year.  Specific
projects have custom schedules that are phased as they reach certain gate stages of
development in the sanction process.
4.
Please see Attachment 1.
5.
For AMI, please see Exhibit ___ (AMIP-3).  For Tools & Equipment the costs and
benefits of the projects and programs were included in the whitepapers in Exhibit __
(GIOP-4).
6.
Please see Attachment 1.
7.
With respect to AMI, as noted in response to Question 3 above, the first two years of the
project are focused on the back-office systems.  The Company does not anticipate
realizing benefits until after the AMI meters and gas modules are installed beginning in
fiscal year (“FY”) 2025.  Please see Exhibit___(AMIP-4) for additional detail on the
benefits calculations in FY 2025 and FY 2026.
For Tools & Equipment, where the historical/unit costs are utilized to forecast a project
or program, the Rate Year forecast has embedded any savings, or conversely any
increases, in those costs.  As these costs were already embedded, the Company is unable
to break out cost details to show specific savings.  For specific projects, detailed savings
projects are only available once a project reaches applicable gate stages of the sanction
process.
8.
With respect to AMI, the project remains pending, subject to consideration by the
Commission.  Although the AMI proposal was not approved on the Commission’s
October agenda, as assumed for purposes of the rate case forecasts, the Company will
seek to launch the project in line with its rate case assumptions, if the Commission
approves the AMI proposal at its November 2020 session.

particular project or program within the Tools & Equipment – Various category.

Response:  
1.
a.
As discussed with DPS Staff, incremental FTE positions reflected in Exhibit___ 
(GIOP-6CU) can exist at both Niagara Mohawk and Service Company.  The 
Service Company provides services to all National Grid USA companies, and 
therefore Service Company employees can charge time to multiple operating 
companies, including Niagara Mohawk.  To determine the FTEs for Service 
Company positions that are relevant to work for Niagara Mohawk would require a 
significant effort of time and resources to analyze hours charged by various 
Service Company employees over multiple years.  Therefore, Attachment 1 
presents Niagara Mohawk employees only (i.e., who charge 100 percent of their 
Exhibit __ (GIOP-11R)

FTEs in Exhibit___(GIOP-6CU).

(GIOP-6CU). 
g.
Ongoing review of Company facilities is conducted to determine the need for
work stations and work station enhancements.  At this time, there are no plans to
retire work stations.
h. – j. See Attachment 2.
k.
Attachment 1 provides a forecast of retirements in the same positions requested as
the incremental FTEs in Exhibit ___ (GIOP-6CU) for Niagara Mohawk only and
does not include forecast retirements for Service Company for the same reasons
discussed in Company’s response to question (a) above.
l.
The Company’s rate case filing is reviewed and approved by a senior executive
steering committee prior to submittal.  This serves as a commitment to hire the
employees approved in the rate case settlement.
m.
Please see the Company’s response to DPS-473 for information on why a FTE

and does not include Service Company employees for the same reasons discussed

Company is providing staffing levels for seven years going back to 2013.  Prior to
2013, the Company used a different general ledger, payroll, and other sub-ledger
systems, and operated under a different cost center hierarchy, so to obtain the
staffing levels for those years would require a significant effort, time, and
resources.

Damage Prevention Supervisor- Responsible for Supervising the Locate and Mark-out of our undergrond assets, managing the Damage Prevention Adviors outreach 
program, damage investigations, and field outreach to third-party excavators. 
One-call Ticket volume increasing trend.  Increase of third-party damages requires additional 
Damage Prevention Advisors (DPAs) in the field to visit more high risk construction jobs.  
Additional support needed to reduce and educate repeat offenders.  More complex 
constructions projects are going on such as Water and Sewer that requires the Damage 
Prevention Advisors to remain onsite for longer periods of time in order to educate Third 
Party Excavators to try to reduce damages. The additional FTE will be required to direct and 
supervise our DPAs.  In addition the FTE would conduct damage investigations, supervise our 
locate & mark-out contractors and provide education outreach to third-party excavators.

High Emitter Leak Repairs
The proposed employees will be

This is a new program.
We estimate that the Company will need to repair 50 additional leaks discovered by this process per 
year. The average amount of time it takes to repair a leak is 53 hours. The total hours needed to repair 
the 50 leaks is 2,650. A full-time FTE can work 2,080 hours (52 weeks x 40 hours per week), factoring in 
non-productive time (i.e. vacation, holiday, sick, and training time) the average productive time 
available to work is approximately 1,600 hours. Based on this information we determined that 
approximately 2 FTE’s would be needed to repair 50 additional high emitter leaks per year.
It will reduce the volume of methane emissions.
Reduced methane emissions will improve air quality.

Job Purpose
To supervise a team of field operations personnel, working to ensure the safe and reliable installation, operation and maintenance of pressure regulators, instrument 
systems, gas transmission pipelines, large industrial metering installations and gas conditioning equipment.
Key Accountabilities
Direct supervision of pressure, instrument and gas conditioning crews relating to all planned work and emergency situations.
Ensure code compliance with all relevant codes of work, company policies and procedures including annual inspections of equipment and facilities.
Assure that information related to maintenance, operation and training activities and changes to procedures and equipment are up-to-date and accurately recorded.
Train a well motivated, competent workforce capable of carrying out the various tasks necessary to maintain the safe, reliable and economic operations of all 
equipment using prepared, up-to-date training programs.
Conduct after hour call outs involving all of the above activities.
Contribute to the safe, reliable and efficient operations of facilities by identifying necessary improvements, modifications and repairs.
Contribute to the preparation and department operations of capital budgets.
Qualifications
Bachelor's degree in engineering and/or a minimum of 5 years experience with instrumentation and regulation functions
Continuing increases of work volume related customers, regulatory compliance, support for 
ongoing construction work, process safety, and general administration

At the time of the cost determination, there was an estimated 472,744 regulators installed in the 
system. Dividing evenly across a 20 year inspection interval resulted in 23,639 inspections per year. A 
benchmark of 65 minutes per inspections was utilized to determine a total annual inspection time of 
25,609 hours per year. With an average of 1,720 working hours available a year per employee, National 
Grid calculated 14.9 or 15 employees would be required per year. 1 additional supervisor was also 
added to manage the work and tracking of this program.

DPS 829
Syracuse, NY

See DPS 829.
Smart meters possess the ability to locally monitor pressure, temperature and flow of 
gas. The smart meter will close the gas service if any preprogrammed threshold is 
exceeded with these variables, such as high flow or overpressurization. The service will 
close and through the AMI network, the Compoany will be notified and respond 
immediately.. Smart RMD's will not have ability to close service, but can instantly 
notify the customer, emergency services and the Company of a potential gas leak, 
allowing the company to respond immediately. Each of these actions increase safety 
and complaince for the customer and the workforce
As mentioned in Part O., implementation of these devices will increase 
safety and compliance to our customers and workforce. Processing meters 
prior to that benefit is realized is required, to ensure the meters are 
working properly once received (inbound in- testing and alarm/event 
verification), as well as routine sample testing.  
As mentioned in Part O., implementation of these devices will 
increase safety and compliance to our customers and workforce. 
Processing meters prior to that benefit is realized is required, to 
ensure the meters are working properly once received (inbound in- 
testing and alarm/event verification), as well as routine sample 
testing.

Project Development can be thought of as early project management.  By aggressively addressing and being able to have dedicated focus on the complex project 
issues earlier, the Project Developer then transfers a job to a Project Manager to execute Final Design and Construction according to this more mature plan.  The 
goal is to have a fully developed plan so that costly design changes do not occur during final design or construction phase.  The project Developer works to fully 
develop the preferred option which means providing, a baseline scope, schedule, resource plans, execution plans, risk assessments with mitigation plans, stakeholder 
& procurement strategies and full costs for sanction before progressing the job for execution.

0 Workload is at an all time high for every FTE in UNY. To comfortably meet our 
compliance criteria, additional overtime and intradepartmental help is needed. 
Beyond our department's scope of work, additional work for intergrity and I&R 
has increased.
Each FTE is responsible for; annual, bi-monthly and 10 year inspections (Distribution pipe, Transmission pipe, Isolated Services, Rectifiers, and Bonds), troubleshooting 
those areas  that are out of compliance, coordination with Gas Ops or the contractor to remediate problem areas, integrity verification work, As-built/ design work 
with engineers for all new steel being installed, data entry, field audits,etc.
The PHMSA plastic rule will be adding a new survey program into our department,  increasing 
the amount of inspections added to UNY's already 30,000 / yr total. Our FTE's have also been 
utilized for I&R's locating program (verifying depth and location of pipe). Now with the new 
Voluntary Integrity Management Program, our FTEs will be the ones performing the work in 
the field.
GBE's new software roll-out, Maximo and Salesforce, will be adding 
additional work to the FTE's day. This software will have to be used 
everyday and will be crucial in how we capture our inspections and 
complinace data.Training on the new software will take some time.

Pipeline Safety Management – Stakeholder Engagement
Regulatory Specialist
0.2
0
0 Workload for FTEs is fully engaged with pipeline safety / public safety issues, 
such as supporting community safety communications, Dig Safe / 811 
promotion, work with public officials and emergency first responders to address 
pipeline safety issues.  Regulatory / rate agreement-driven programs, such as 
Residential Methane Detector distribution, is handled across the team above 
and beyond what the positions are already assigned to manage.
- Develop and maintain close working relationships with City, Town, and local police and fire departments. 
- Work collaboratively with the Jurisdictional Team and operational teams and provide technical point of contact / focal point for assisting with education services for 
gas pipeline safety programs / concerns.
- Develop and maintain close working relationships with internal departments, such as Learning and Development, Gas Operations, Electric Operations, Emergency 
Planning, Government Relations and the Jurisdictional teams and provide assistance and guidance as it pertains to gas and electric safety awareness. 
- Maintain knowledge of all aspects of the Pipeline Safety Public Awareness Program as well as current and emerging regulatory issues affecting the industry 
including as a minimum API RP-1162.
- Balance the needs of the City, Town, local police and fire departments and other emergency response agencies with National Grid’s interests.
- Work closely with allied trade organizations, Gas Marketing and Materials & Standards teams to engage and educate trades (HVAC, Plumbing, Electrical, 
Construction, Demolition, Landscaping, Excavators etc.). 
- Work closely with all emergency response agencies and other municipal / public entities to identify gas and electric safety awareness gaps and work collaboratively

concerns with gas and electric public safety.

Smart meters possess the ability to locally monitor pressure, temperature and flow of 
gas. The smart meter will close the gas service if any preprogrammed threshold is 
exceeded with these variables, such as high flow or overpressurization. The service will 
close and through the AMI network, the Compoany will be notified and respond 
immediately.. Smart RMD's will not have ability to close service, but can instantly 
notify the customer, emergency services and the Company of a potential gas leak, 
allowing the company to respond immediately. Each of these actions increase safety 
and complaince for the customer and the workforce
The MDS and AMO analysts will be monitoring and managing the data 
coming across the network, associated with smart meters and smart RMD's, 
and adjusting billing as neccesary. The MDS analysts will ensure the end-to-
end communication system is reliable to each and every customers targeted 
for a Smart Meter and Smart RMD. Ensuring connecticitivty reinforces the 
strength of the network, and the likelihood that the Company will recieve 
the alarm/event from the smart devices. If a service is closed off, the AMO 
analyst will work with the customer to ensure fair and accurate willing.

0 Responsibilities include but are not limited too: Owning  and executing Capital

Capital engineers are responsible for planning and project support for all capital programs in the Pressure Regulation Engineering portfolio. This includes the Heater, 
Pressure Regulation Facilities, System Automation, Special Projects, Overpressure Protection, Gate Station Overpressure Protection, and Transmission Station 
Integrity programs. Additionally they support I&R requests, and often facilitate work from the I&R reactive program. PRE Capital Engineers are also projected to 
provide support for proposed I&R programs, such as the Oil Seal Replacement Program.
PHMSA's ruling on October 1st, 2019 dictated the need for TVC records for all transmission 
facilities by 2035. To comply with this ruling National Grid forecasts an increase in capital 
replacement projects by three annually.  Additionally National Gird has developed an 
overpressure protection program in response to the incident in the Merrimack Valley in 2018. 
This proposed FTE will support the project development and field execution of these new 
programs. Additionally, PRE capital engineers have seen an increased volume of work in their 
day-to-day activities due to the utilization of new capital investment tools and the 
implementation of project development framework. These factors have increased the design 
and development time that the capital planning engineers need to dedicate to individual 
projects in order to fully optimize designs and field execution. PRE also supports programs and 
requests by I&R, so additional programs such as the proposed oil seal replacement program 
will require engineering and support from PRE.

National Grid's TSIP proposes adding three transmission rebuilds to the project portfolio annually. In 
addition to the onset of the Overpressure Protection Program has identified 24 sites that should be 
addressed to minimize system risk. Additional programs such as the oil seal replacement program will 
also require PRE support. In order to quanitfy the labor hours associated with these proposed programs, 
the company used similar recent historical projects to quantify the labor hours required. The total hours 
identified equate to one FTE in PRE's capital planning team.
Two of the new programs this FTE is identified to support are in place to ensure 
compliance with federal rulemaking and safe operation of our systems.  The FTE will be 
responsible for the execution of capital replacement work directly tied to PHMSA's 
rulemaking regarding TVC records. The Overpressure Protection Program execution is 
also reliant upon the approval of this FTE. This program seeks to implement override 
systems and overpressure protection at stations identified with higher operating risks 
(third-party damage, outdated design, etc.).
Customers will benefit from having an additional FTE as it will ensure PRE's 
capital portfolio is executred in a timely and cost effective manner. The 
programs within PRE's portfolio serve to ensure compliance with federal and 
state codes as well as increase the overall safety and reliability our station 
assets throughout the system.

Transmission Station Integrity Program
Engineer - IMP
1
0
0 The proposed FTE will be responsible for the integrity assessment of an 
anticipated 17 transmission facilities annually across the the NMPC territory, 
This includes pre-assessment data collection, field execution, 
repair/replacement oversight, and data intetegration into the station integrity 
program. The FTE will also be responsible for the validation/execution of 
triennial risk assessment information on National Grid's 428 stations and 42 
heaters across the NMPC territory.
Integrity Management Engineers are responsible for compiling risk assessment data for stations within the sytem. They validate data with field inspections and 
compile an integrity assessment schedule using a risk based approach. They will be responsible for  integrity management activities at stations including the 
onboarding of contractors and coordination with internal parties. They will collect data from their assessments and upon the discovery of defects they will be 
responsible for overseeing the corrective actions. The information discovered through the integrity management activies will be used to improve the integrity 
program and collaborate with the capital planning team on replacement schedules.
This is a new program intended to comply with federal code requirements.

National Grid has determined the need to perform assessments on roughly 17 stations annually on a 
recurring cycle. This work includes reviewing as-built information,  station operating history, corrosion 
history, performing pre-assessment inspection(s),  performing/overseeing assessment activities, and 
integrating data findings into the station integrity management program. Durations for these 
inspections may vary depending on the facility complexity and findings during the assessments. The 
company estimated on average a station assessment from inception to completion will take three 
weeks.
This program ensures National Grid is proactively identifying threats to our station 
assets. These actions will help ensure the safe and reliable delivery of natural gas to 
our customers. The work from this FTE will also ensure compliance with federal 
requirements for performing integrity assessments on transmission assets.
Customers will benefit from having an FTE supporting integrity assessments 
in the field. These assessments proactively identify threats before failure. 
Discovering threats proactively, helps ensure safe and reliable service to our 
customers. There is also a cost savings associated with identifying and 
addressing threats before they become emergent or cause failure.

The Company is using Advanced Leak Detection (ALD) technology to discover high emitters that need to be eliminated in a timely manner.
High emitter leak repairs are estimated to 
consist of 24% CAPEX and 76% OPEX.
Currently, I&R is being Supervised by a single Supervisor in each geographic area of Upstate (Syracuse, Watertown, Utica, Albany, Gloversville/Saratoga.  Supervisors are completely responsible in their area of assignment 
for managing and operating pressure reducing stations, gate stations, transmission pipelines, large industrial metering sites, customer facing work for commercial customers, supporting construction activities associated with 
Transmission Pipeline Integrity Management, Main Replacement, New Customer connections, and the management and oversight of local Union employees.  Local I&R Supervisors are also responsible for managing all off-
hours emergencies and system problems as a result of notifications from Gas Control.  On average, I&R Supervisors are working 60+ hour work-weeks in addition to off-hours and weekend system problems.  The vast 
majority of work beyond 40 hours per week is not eligible for overtime pay.  The large workload and associated work hours result in significant "quality of life" implications for I&R Supervisors and makes retention of these 
individuals difficult.
I&R Supervisors are responsible for managing and operating complex transmission and distribution networks and gas facilities that contain very complicated gas processing equipment and instrumentation.  Many of these 
facilities benefit from formal Engineering training and education.  Failure to properly maintain and operate these facilities has the potential to result in catastrophic failures, significant cost impacts to both the Company and 
Customers, and severe Regulatory Compliance implications. 
Currently, I&R has no means to back up any of the local I&R Supervisors for times when the normal Supervisor is absent or occupied with other Company business.  This results in a situation where the Union workforce is 
essentially unsupervised and acting upon their own knowledge and experience, increasing the risk that errors could be made while working on complicated systems.  Additionally, institutional knowledge and experience  is 
concentrated in a small number of individuals and increases the risk to the Company and Customers should a Supervisor retire, become ill, or take a different role within the Company.  Even for highly qualified individuals 
with Engineering or other educational backgrounds, it will require a minimum of 3 years to become proficient in I&R systems, tasks, and requirements.  The lack of a means to duplicate I&R Supervisory knowledge and 
experience poses a significant safety and operational risk to both the Company and Customers.
Accordingly, the Company is requesting the addition of 2 incremental I&R Supervisors, one each in the Syracuse and Albany areas, to help mitigate the quality of life issues for existing Supervisors, to replicate institutional 
knowledge in a larger group of employees, to be ready to respond to the loss of one of the current Supervisors, and to reduce the operational and safety risks to the Company and Customers by reasonably expanding the 
base of I&R Supervisory oversight for a highly critical role.
I&R Supervisors support several Capital 
programs including the I&R Proactive, Gas 
Transmission Pipeline Integrity, Regulator 
Station Integrity, Complex Construction and 
Capital Delivery, and other programs where 
pipeline system management and outage 
control is involved.
Industry events such as Silver Springs, Maryland in which a failed service regulator and disconnected vent line resulted in fatalities has emphasized the need to expand the scope of National Grid's current multi-meter 
regulator inspections to single-meter regulator inspections.

Khan
Unit/Unit Cost
The unit cost of the future years is expected 
to increase due to the shift of inspection 
types as the phase of the project matures. 
The first phase involves mainline inspections 
which are approximately $500/unit. The 
second phase involves push inspections 
which is approximately $1000/unit. The final 
phase of the project involves test pits which 
are $3000/unit. ratio of mainline to push 
inspection shifting towards push inspections 
which are more expensive. The push 
inspection unit price is approximately double 
that of a mainline inspection. Due to the 
unknown scope of inspections which will 
require an additional test hole to complete 
the inspection

data from FY18 to FY20 to develop budget.

Transmission Station Integrity
912,000
 
3,007,000
 
12,928,000
        
13,187,000
        
13,451,000
        
Greco
Project Estimate
This program considered the following factor: 
on October 1, 2019, USDOT issued the final 
rules relative to the proposed rulemakings, 
with an effective date of July 1, 2020. The 
mandates, advisory bulletin, notices, and 
final rules also apply to transmission-
connected pressure regulating stations and 
take stations (hereafter, ‘stations’). The 
Transmission Station Integrity Program (TSIP), 
consists of projects which focus on the 
rehabilitation, partial replacement, or full 
replacement of stations that do not meet the 
requirements of MAOP reconfirmation as set 
forth in the final rule. By July 2, 2035, the 
Company must attain 100 percent compliance 
for all transmission assets. 
N/A 
N/A 
This program budget was developed by a 
combination of historical transmission 
station project estimates and the Gas Project 
Estimating CostBook Tool (Microsoft Access). 
Within the last 3 years Transmission Station 
projects has been routed through the Capital 
Delivery Process. This process distributes the 
spend on a project across 4 years; Y1 - Project 
Development, Y2 - Design/Procurement, Y3 - 
Procurement/Construction, Y4 - Project 
Closeout.
Five years of historic data is not 
representative of cost increases that have 
occured over the past years due to increased 
business costs, execution costs, and 
development costs, therefore 3 years of 
historical data is a more accurate 
representation. The historical spend that this 
budget was partly based on is from the 
following; Elton Ave GRS (FY18/19) - 
$4,793,3030, Alplaus GRS (FY19) - $3,612,264, 
Mariaville GRS (FY20) - $2,441,398, and 
Brookview GRS (FY20) - $4,409,486. 
FY18, FY19, and FY20 Transmission Station 
projects were referenced to develop the Rate 
Year Budget.

N/A
This program budget was developed by a 
combination of historical transmission 
station project estimates and the Gas Project 
Estimating CostBook Tool (Microsoft Access). 
Within the last 3 years Transmission Station 
projects has been routed through the Capital 
Delivery Process. This process distributes the 
spend on a project across 4 years; Y1 - Project 
Development, Y2 - Design/Procurement, Y3 - 
Procurement/Construction, Y4 - Project 
Closeout.
Five years of historic data is not 
representative of cost increases that have 
occured over the past years due to increased 
business costs, execution costs, and 
development costs, therefore 3 years of 
historical data is a more accurate 
representation. The historical spend that this 
budget was partly based on is from the 
following; Elton Ave GRS (FY18/19) - 
$4,793,3030, Alplaus GRS (FY19) - $3,612,264, 
Mariaville GRS (FY20) - $2,441,398, and 
Brookview GRS (FY20) - $4,409,486. 
FY18, FY19, and FY20 Transmission Station 
projects were referenced to develop the Rate 
Year Budget.

Meas. & Reg
Gate Station Over Pressure Protection
720,000
 
2,499,000
 
1,872,000
          
- 
- 
Greco
Project Estimate
This funding considers the requirement to 
have National Grid Company-owned over 
pressure protection at all 24 take stations. 
Currently 19 of 24 stations require addition

Meas. & Reg
Odorant Vapor Management at Gate Stations 
- 
50,000
 
200,000
             
200,000
             
150,000
             
Zielinski
Project Estimate
In the event of a spill or release of 
mercaptan, this project's intention is to 
install specialized equipment that will 
contain mercaptan odors to inside the 
odorant injection facility .  Raw odorant is 
purposely meant to be extremely potent 
which allows the public the ability to smell 
low volume gas leaks.  However, performing 
maintenance on odorant injection equipment 
becomes difficult due the nature of such a 
strong odor, increasing the risk of large 
numbers of false odor complaints should 
vapors escape to the ambient air.   National 
Grid has investigated options available 
throughout the industry to handle this issue.  
The project is comprised of three main 
initiatives; the installation of a vacuum 
filtration device, sealing the concrete floors 
to allow for proper clean up of spills, and the 
installation of mercaptan detector to allow 
Gas Control to remotely detect mercaptan 
releases.   
A unit cost analysis was not used to derive 
this project
A unit cost analysis was not used to derive 
this project
There are no recent historical costs 
associated with conducting this type of work.  
Therefore, vendor quotes were obtained for 
the vacuum filtration device.  The labor 
associated with the installation was 
estimated.  Estimates were also used for the 
sealing of the floors and for the methane 
detection equipment. refer to Schedule F.
A historical spend was not used to derive this 
project
A historical spend was not used to derive this 
project

Meas. & Reg
Critical Spares
- 
500,000
 
250,000
             
150,000
             
100,000
             
Zielinski
Project Estimate
National Grid's gas infrastructure contains a 
large variety of components and equipment, 
many of which are complex or designed 
specifically for individual locations or 
purposes.  In order to ensure reliability and 
system safety, the Company must maintain a 
sufficient inventory of complex or long lead 
time compents to manage emergency 
situations or to facilitate timely repairs.  This 
initiative will supply the necessary funding to 
ensure long lead time or complex gas system 
components are readily available.
A unit cost analysis was not used to derive 
this project
A unit cost analysis was not used to derive 
this project
During the development of this project a list 
of long lead time equipment was developed.  
From this list, items were then evaluated as 
to their criticality.  Items that were deemed 
crucial for the continuous flow of gas to the 
customers were sectionalized in a list that 
would be included in this program.  Costs for 
the program were estimated using quotes, 
worksheets, and historical spending.  The 
intent of the program is to sequester long 
lead time and complex components for use 
during emergencies.
A historical spend was not used to derive this 
project
A historical spend was not used to derive this 
project
Meas. & Reg
Oil Seal Replacement
- 
1,640,000
 
1,640,000
          
1,640,000
          
1,640,000
          
Zielinski
Project Estimate
This initiative was developed to replace aging 
and outdated oil seals.  Oil seals are a form of 
overpressure protection that uses the weight 
of oil to contain gas pressure inside of a pipe.  
In the event of an overpressure situation an 
the subsequent operation of an oil seal, the 
oil is displaced and allows gas to safely 
release to the atmosphere keeping pipeline 
pressure at safe levels.  Oil seals do not have 
the ability to automatically close and must be 
manually reset, even if the overpressure 
condition is resolved.  Replacing oils seals 
with newer forms of overpressure protection 
that will modulate and automatically reset 
reduces emissions of methane and oily 
aerosols to atmosphere and provide 
improved environmental benefits.
A unit cost analysis was not used to derive 
this project
A unit cost analysis was not used to derive 
this project
This estimate was created by National Grid's 
estimating department using National Grid's 
Gas Project Estimating Tool.  
A historical spend was not used to derive this 
project
A historical spend was not used to derive this 
project

Exhibit 5
Pipeleine 16, Orange Commons
Pipeline 34 was recently completed, a 10 mile replacement,cross country, undeveloped area, 10-inch pipe, largely reused the same right-of-way
Cost of PL34 was approximately $6M/mile
Pipeline 16, Orange Commons is 24-inch diameter pipe, approximately 6000 feet which crosses a State highwayand then runs througha brand new suburban development
Increased costs due to larger diameter pipe, new more equipment ,more welders,difficult construction if reusing the same ROW, costs to aquire new right-of-way, state high way crossing

ESTIMATING DOCUMENT 
Page 1 of 3 
Estimate Overview 
Version 06.1 –  
03/14/2019
C081284-East Canada Creek HDD Dolgeville-
4.4 
PURPOSE 
This form documents the assumptions and any other items that went into creating the estimate.  To ensure 
consistency in all the published estimates, the items that influenced the final estimate are listed here and then 
included in the published Estimate PDF file. 
1.0 
ESTIMATE  DESCRIPTION 
1.1 
Project Funding # : 
C081284 
1.2 
Project Work Order #: 
10028406579 
1.3 
Estimate Name: 
C081284-East Canada Creek HDD Dolgeville-4.4 
1.4 
Estimate Completion Date: 08/19/2020 
1.5 
Estimate Stage: 
4.4 
2.0 
SCOPE OF WORK  
Perform a 688’ HDD under East Canada Creek and install a 4” steel distribution main. Transition to 4” 
plastic distribution main from the entry pit to the tie-in locations. Open trench from the entry/exit pits to the 
tie-in locations. Street opening will occur on 2nd Street on both sides of the creek. 
3.0 
CLARIFICATIONS 
3.1 
The estimate is based on drawing D-70374-C dated 05/16/2019. 
3.2 
AFUDC was calculated using a combined debt/equity rate of 6.10% which is based on the past 2 
years average actual rate and an in-service date of 09/30/2020. 
3.3 
The estimate includes $206,473 spend to date as of 08/03/2020.  
3.4 
The project scope meets the NYS PSC Complex Project definition and will be a candidate for the 
NYS UNY Gas Estimating Performance Metric. 
3.5 
Civil, mechanical and restoration work will be completed by John Anderson Construction Inc.  Their 
total final award was $792,610.  ($774,610 (Original bid) + $18,000 (Change Order)  
3.6 
There is a change order of $18,000 included in this estimate from Seth Herman’s email to extend 
the drill 50’ dated August 14th,2020. 
3.7 
Ten (10) percent of all welds will be testing using Non-Destructive Examination (NDE). 
3.8 
National Grid’s overhead and burden rates used in this estimate were based on the May 2020 Sage 
Templates. 
3.9 
Material pricing is based on actual PO Costs and National Grid inventory items. 
3.10 320 ft of the 4” Powercrete Pipe was transferred from excess material at a zero cost. 
Exhibit __ (GIOP-11R)

ESTIMATING DOCUMENT 
Page 2 of 3 
Estimate Overview 
Version 06.1 –  
03/14/2019
C081284-East Canada Creek HDD Dolgeville-
4.4 
4.0 
ASSUMPTIONS 
4.1 
The field construction activities are scheduled for 45 days in duration. 
4.2 
John Anderson Construction Inc. will be working 12-hour days/11 days on/3 days off. 
4.3 
Construction will be complete by October 15th, 2020.  
4.4 
One Full-Time National Grid FCC will be On-Site during construction activities. 
4.5 
A second National Grid FCC will be On-Site for 10 days during HDD activities. 
4.6 
Kleinfelder Supervision will be On-Site for all drilling activities and for vibration monitoring. 
4.7 
One 4” Steel HDD for 688 ft. 
4.8 
Two (2) 4” Tie-Ins with using 4” side-out fittings will be performed by National Grid in-house 
welders. 
5.0 
EXCLUSIONS 
5.1 
Additional costs for unanticipated work hour restrictions. 
5.2 
No additional cost during Winter is included which leads to less productivity. 
5.3 
Removal and disposal of hazardous spoils. 
5.4 
Dewatering. 
5.5 
SWPPP. 
5.6 
Major downtime due to weather. 
5.7 
Drilling through rock. 
5.8 
On-site safety person as the award was under $1 Million. 
6.0 
RISK / CONTINGENCY 
The risk-based contingency figures (P50/P80) presented in this estimate are intended to cover risks 
associated with the presented scope. Scope changes are not included. 
6.1 
The estimate includes a contingency of 5%. 
6.2 
A risk meeting was held on 07/28/2020. 
6.2.1 
Risk Dollars P50 / EV - $57,441 
6.2.2 
Risk Dollars P80 / SC - $116,244 
7.0 
ESCALATION 
N/A

ESTIMATING DOCUMENT 
Page 1 of 4 
Estimate Overview 
Version 06.1 –  
03/14/2019
C082363-RTE 146 Rexford-4.3 
PURPOSE 
This form documents the assumptions and any other items that went into creating the estimate.  To ensure 
consistency in all the published estimates, the items that influenced the final estimate are listed here and then 
included in the published Estimate PDF file. 
1.0 
ESTIMATE  DESCRIPTION 
1.1 
Project Funding # : 
C082363, C085152 (Land) 
1.2 
Project Work Order #: 
10029154763, 90000200602, 90000209245 (Land) 
1.3 
Estimate Name: 
C082363-RTE 146 Rexford-4.3 
1.4 
Estimate Completion Date: 08/18/2020 
1.5 
Estimate Stage: 
4.3 
2.0 
SCOPE OF WORK  
This project is needed to maintain above threshold minimum pressure on the 45 PSIG system. Without this 
project, 4,100 customers would experience pressures below the minimum threshold pressure when design day 
conditions (-10°F) occur. 
The project consists of the following major components: 
•
Installation of a new 124 PSIG to 45 PSIG Gas Pressure Regulator Station.
•
Installation of 240’ of 16” 45 PSIG steel gas main.
•
Installation of 14,370’ of 12” 124 PSIG coated steel gas main.
3.0 
CLARIFICATIONS 
3.1 
The estimate is based on the Gas Distribution Scoping Document dated 06/26/2020 and Drawing 
Numbers D-48737-E (Main) dated 06/30/2020 and D-48735-E (GRS) dated 09/09/2019. 
3.2 
AFUDC was calculated using a combined debt/equity rate of 6.10% which is based on the past 2 
  years average actual rate and an in-service date of 11/01/2021. 
3.3 
The estimate includes $657,110 spend to date as of 08/03/2020.  
3.4 
Civil, mechanical and restoration activities will be performed by a contractor awarded through a 
competitive bidding process. 
3.5 
A miscellaneous equipment allowance of $27,700 was included in this estimate. 
3.6 
This estimate includes a transfer of $108 from work order 9000020601 that will be cancelled. 
3.7 
The project scope meets the NYS PSC complex project definition and will be candidate for the NYS 
PSC UNY Gas Estimating Performance Metric. 
3.8 
Ten (10) percent of all welds will be tested using Non-Destructive Examination (NDE). 
Exhibit __ (GIOP-11R) 
Page 92 of 145


--- Page 140 ---
ESTIMATING DOCUMENT 
Page 2 of 4 
Estimate Overview 
Version 06.1 –  
03/14/2019
C082363-RTE 146 Rexford-4.3 
3.9 
National Grid’s overhead and burden rates used in this estimate were based on the May 2020 
SAGE Templates. 
3.10 Stock material pricing is based on SAGE Estimating database inventory items.  Non-stock material 
costs were obtained from vendor quotes or actual costs from past projects. 
3.11 The Means and Methods for construction includes, Horizontal Directional drilling (HDD), Jack & 
Bore and Open Cut trench. 
3.12 The project requires the completion of a SWPPP along with weekly inspections. 
3.13 Tree clearing will not be part of the competitive bid. 
3.14 Work order 90000209245 for the 20 Land Easements was created for $375,000. 
3.15 Odorant injection and monitoring will be required and performed by National Grid I&R Crew. 
4.0 
ASSUMPTIONS 
4.1 
The 24-week construction duration is based upon the preliminary construction schedule. 
4.2 
The project will be started and completed in Fiscal Year 2022. 
4.3 
The Contractor’s weekly work hours consist of working six (6) days per week and 12 hours per day. 
4.4 
Crew productivity for the open trench portion of this project assumes 120 ft of steel gas main 
installed per day. 
4.5 
The total installed pipe length does not exceed 14,730 ft. (14,370’ Main W/O and 360’ GRS W/O. 
4.6 
The open cut trench length does not exceed 8,566 ft (8,326 of 12” ST and 240 ft of 16” ST main.  
4.7 
Three (3) separate horizontal directional drill segments required.  The total length of main installed 
by HDD will be 5,979 ft of 12” ST gas main. 
4.8 
Three (3) separate Jack and Bore segments required.  The total length of main installed by Jack & 
Bore will be 185 ft of 12” ST gas main. 
4.9 
The wetlands and marshaling yards will require the placement of 1,639 mats. 
4.10 5.3 acres of tree of clearing. 
4.11 There will be One (1) Main Tie-In consisting of One (1) 12” TDW Steel 3 Way Fitting. 
4.12 Three (3) Full-Time Contractor Supervisors required during construction activities. 
4.13 One (1) Full-Time Electrical Spotter required for the duration of the project. 
4.14 One (1) Full-Time On-Site Safety Person required for the duration of the project. 
4.15 National Grid will require a 3rd party Contractor (Kleinfelder) on site for additional oversight during 
the  HDD activities.  
4.16 Work Area protection and traffic control costs for the duration of the project.  
4.17 Pressure test will be pneumatic and not a hydrotest.

5.0 
EXCLUSIONS 
5.1 
Permit stipulations requiring reduced day time or night time work hours. 
5.2 
Additional labor costs for working during the winter months. 
5.3 
Delay costs for the handling of any environmental hazardous spoils. 
5.4 
Delay costs for the productivity loses for major weather events. 
5.5 
Delay costs for contractor losses with unsuccessful HDD bores or consolidated rock being 
encountered. 
5.6 
National Grid internal policy change requiring a Certified Welding Inspector (CWI) be present for all 
welding activities.  
5.7 
Dewatering. 
5.8 
Environmental Monitoring. 
5.9 
Scar Guard on the HDD Pipe. 
6.0 
RISK / CONTINGENCY 
The risk-based contingency figures (P50/P80) presented in this estimate are intended to cover risks 
associated with the presented scope. Scope changes are not included. 
6.1 
The estimate includes a contingency of 5%. 
6.2 
A risk meeting was held on 07/14/2020.

6.2.2

ESTIMATING DOCUMENT 
Page 1 of 4 
Estimate Overview 
Version 06.1 –  
03/14/2019
C082726-RTE 146 Altamont (Rev 1)-4.3
PURPOSE 
This form documents the assumptions and any other items that went into creating the estimate.  To ensure 
consistency in all the published estimates, the items that influenced the final estimate are listed here and then 
included in the published Estimate PDF file. 
1.0 
ESTIMATE  DESCRIPTION 
1.1 
Project Funding # :     
     C082726 
1.2 
Project Work Order #:       
 10028693132, 90000206305 (Land) 
1.3 
Estimate Name:     
  C082726-RTE 146 Altamont (Rev 1)-4.3 
1.4 
Estimate Completion Date: 
08/31/2020 
1.5 
Estimate Stage: 
4.3 
2.0 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Route 146 Altamont reinforcement project located in Altamont, NY ensures the Albany 45 PSIG gas 
distribution system maintains a threshold minimum design pressure of 10 PSI during design day operations.  
The Synergy Model using the forecasted load growth projections in the area predict more than 1,100 
customers may be impacted by below minimum gas pressures.  
The project consists of the following major components: 
•
Installation of 7,965’  of 8” plastic main.
•
Installation of  2,465’ of 8” Powercrete coated steel main
•
Installation of 2,397’ of 12” plastic gas main
•
Retirement of 1,075’ of 4” plastic gas main
•
Retirement of 2,582’of 12” steel gas main
•
Retirement of 122’ of 6” Steel gas main
•
Relay, reconnect or insert 24 gas service lines
3.0 
CLARIFICATIONS   
3.1 
The estimate is based on the Gas Distribution Scoping Document dated March 23rd, 2020 and 
Profile Drawings State Route 146 dated 03/23/20 and Profile Drawings Western Ave drawings 
dated 04/06/2020.(Only Profile Pages 1 and 2) 
3.2 
AFUDC was calculated using a combined debt/equity rate of 6.10% which is based on the past   
2-year average actual rate and an in-service date of 10/31/2022.
3.3 
The estimate includes $1,160,149 spend to date as of 08/11/2020. ($869,168.06 work order 
10028693132 and $290,981.30 Land work order 90000206305)  
3.4 
Work order 90000206305 was created for 26 Land Easements for $320,000. 
3.5 
$35,422 from work order 10028693359 will be transferred to work order 10028693132. 
3.6 
The project scope meets the NYS PSC complex project definition and will be candidate for the NYS 
PSC UNY Gas Estimating Performance Metric. 
Exhibit __ (GIOP-11R) 
Page 123 of 145


--- Page 171 ---
ESTIMATING DOCUMENT 
Page 2 of 4 
Estimate Overview 
Version 06.1 –  
03/14/2019
C082726-RTE 146 Altamont (Rev 1)-4.3
3.7 
Civil, mechanical and restoration activities will be performed by a contractor awarded through a 
competitive bidding process. 
3.8 
Ten (10) percent of all welds will be tested using Non-Destructive Examination (NDE). 
3.9 
National Grid’s overhead and burden rates used in this estimate were based on the May 2020 
SAGE Templates. 
3.10 Stock material pricing is based on SAGE Estimating database inventory items.  Non-stock material 
costs were obtained from vendor quotes or actual costs from past projects. 
3.11 The Means and Methods for construction includes both Horizontal Directional drilling (HDD) and 
Open Cut trench methods. 
3.12 Work area protection plans includes two (2) flaggers for the entire duration of the project. 
3.13 The project requires the completion of a SWPPP along with weekly inspections. 
4.0 
ASSUMPTIONS 
4.1 
The field construction activities are scheduled for 16 weeks in duration. 
4.2 
The project will be started and completed in Fiscal Year 2023. 
4.3 
The Contractor’s weekly work hours consist of working six (6) days per week and 8 hours per day. 
4.4 
Crew productivity for the open trench portion of this project assumes 120 ft of gas main installed per 
day. 
4.5 
The total installed pipe length does not exceed 12,827 ft. 
4.6 
The open cut trench length does not exceed 5,510 ft of 8” PE and 2,397 ft of 12” PE main.  
4.7 
The soft surface trench excavation length will be 7,197 ft and hard surface trench excavation length 
will be 710 ft. 
4.8 
Seven (7) separate horizontal directional drill segments required.  The total length of main installed 
by HDD will be 4,920 ft. (2455 ft of 8” PE, 2465 of 8” Steel) 
4.9 
Final road and site restoration activities consists of 400 square ft of concrete sidewalks, 2,210 
square ft of asphalt roadway and 28,788 square feet of soft surface. 
4.10 The wetlands and marshaling yards will require the placement of 820 mats. 
4.11 There will be 2 separate main Tie-Ins consisting of 1 end of main 8” plastic squeeze off and 12” 
Steel Side-Out Fitting. 
4.12 Two Full-Time Contractor Supervisors required  during construction activities. 
4.13 One Full-Time On-Site Safety Person required for the duration of the project. 
4.14 National Grid will require a 3rd party Contractor (Kleinfelder) on site for additional oversight during 
the  HDD activities for steel bore segments only. 
4.15 Main installation outside the public right of way requires 26 individual private property easements 
required. 
4.16 Work Area protection and traffic control costs for 16-week duration. 
4.17 (2) Railroad Flaggers for 3 weeks in duration required during the railroad crossing activities. 
4.18 A preliminary pressure test will be performed every 1500 ft and overall pressure test at the 
completion of the project. 
4.19 24 services need to be tied-over.  Assuming 2 services a day.

5.0 
EXCLUSIONS 
5.1 
Permit stipulations requiring reduced day time or night time work hours. 
5.2 
Additional labor costs for working during the winter months. 
5.3 
Delay costs for the handling of any environmental hazardous spoils. 
5.4 
Delay costs for the productivity loses for major weather events. 
5.5 
Delay costs for contractor losses with unsuccessful HDD bores or consolidated rock being 
encountered. 
5.6 
National Grid internal policy change requiring a Certified Welding Inspector (CWI) be present for all 
welding activities.  
6.0 
RISK / CONTINGENCY 
The risk-based contingency figures (P50/P80) presented in this estimate are intended to cover 
risks associated with the presented scope. Major scope changes are not included. 
6.1 
The estimate includes a contingency of 5%. 
6.2 
A risk meeting was held on 08/26/2020. 
6.2.1 
Risk Dollars P50 / EV - $715,344 
6.2.2 
Risk Dollars P80 / SC - $1,116,261

Pace Energy and Climate Center; Alliance for a Green Economy, 
Priya Pookkulam 
TO: 
 
National Grid, Gas Infrastructure and Operations Panel 
SUBJECT:   Mandated and Reliability Projects - Gas Safety 
Request: 
50.
Regarding the Albany Loop Project, please explain why the Company “proposes to spend
0.750 million in FY 2022 to continue project development and in-progress engineering”
(Testimony of GIOP, pg. 75). Please also provide detailed descriptions of, and a list of
expenditures relating to, “project development” and “in- progress engineering” for the
Albany Loop Project in FY 2022.
Response:  
The project development and in-process engineering expenditures included for the Albany Loop 
Project in fiscal year 2022 were estimates based on information available at the time of the filing, 
including the project’s timeline, and may be subject to change if the project is modified in the 
future.  Project development and in-process engineering costs are a component of all 
construction projects, typically incurred in advance of construction, and are essential to the 
delivery of a project.  As indicated on page 74 of the Gas Infrastructure and Operations Panel’s 
direct testimony, the Company identified the Albany Loop Project as the best option to enhance 
reliability for approximately 50,000 customers in the event of a loss of gas supply and to address 
vulnerability concerns in the East Gate region.  Accordingly, project development and in-process 
engineering costs are required for advancement of the project. 
Project development includes costs associated with permitting activities, such as cultural 
investigations and environmental delineation, and preliminary material and contractor 
procurement functions.  In-progress engineering includes costs associated with responses to 
inquiries, site investigation efforts, other data gathering and analytical work, and the preparation 
of documents and drawings to reflect and/or submit the data collected.
